## A.7 Executive Summary

- **Project Title:** Green Climate Fund Proposal
- **Project Duration:** 6 years
- **Total Budget:** USD 98,146,992
- **Co-financing:** USD 25,396,996
- **Environmental and Social Safeguards:** Yes / No

### A.7.1 Project Description

The project aims to reduce greenhouse gas emissions by implementing renewable energy projects in developing countries. The project will provide financial support for the installation of solar panels, wind turbines, and other renewable energy technologies. The project is expected to create jobs and stimulate economic growth while reducing carbon emissions.

### A.7.2 Project Objectives

- **Objective 1:** Reduce greenhouse gas emissions by 50% within 10 years.
- **Objective 2:** Increase the use of renewable energy sources to 30% of total energy consumption.
- **Objective 3:** Create 10,000 jobs in the renewable energy sector.

### A.7.3 Project Activities

- **Activity 1:** Installation of solar panels
- **Activity 2:** Installation of wind turbines
- **Activity 3:** Training and capacity building for local communities
- **Activity 4:** Monitoring and evaluation of project progress

### A.7.4 Project Results

- **Result 1:** Reduction in greenhouse gas emissions by 50%
- **Result 2:** Increase in renewable energy use to 30% of total consumption
- **Result 3:** Creation of 10,000 jobs in the renewable energy sector

### A.7.5 Project Management

The project will be managed by a team of experienced professionals with expertise in renewable energy and project management. The team will work closely with local communities to ensure that the project is implemented effectively and efficiently.

### A.7.6 Project Monitoring and Evaluation

The project will be monitored and evaluated regularly to ensure that it is achieving its objectives and delivering the expected results. The monitoring and evaluation process will include regular site visits, data collection, and analysis.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V1.2 PAGE 3

The Carbon Trust is currently facing the additional challenge of climate change and environmental sustainability issues. The Carbon Trust has been working with the Green Investment Bank (GIB) to provide green loans and investments.

## Climate: The Borrowing Bench Cost Curve (collectively “Barclays, RBS, and Janus” or the “BBC”) was facing a significant increase in their borrowing costs due to the impact of climate change on their operations. These changes are affecting not only the financials but also the supply of energy resources for the future.

The BBC is looking at ways to reduce their carbon footprint by investing in renewable energy sources, including solar and wind power. The BBC is also considering the use of electric vehicles and other forms of transportation that have a lower impact on the environment.

## Energy: The BBC has been working with the Green Investment Bank (GIB) to provide green loans and investments. The GIB has been providing funding for renewable energy projects, including solar and wind power. The GIB has also been working with the Department of Energy and Climate Change (DECC) to provide funding for energy efficiency programs.

The introduction of FIB models in BRICS is seen as a strategic step forward addressing changes in the financial sector and the need for sustainable energy financing. This will help to reduce the cost of borrowing for renewable energy projects, making it more affordable for companies to invest in green technologies.

The Green Investment Bank (GIB) has been working with Barclays, RBS, and Janus to provide loans and investments in their electricity sector. The GIB has also been providing funding for renewable energy projects, including solar and wind power.

The GIB has been working with the Department of Energy and Climate Change (DECC) to provide funding for energy efficiency programs. The GIB has also been working with the BBC to provide loans and investments in their electricity sector.

The Green Investment Bank (GIB) is a key player in the green finance market, providing loans and investments for renewable energy projects. The GIB has been working with Barclays, RBS, and Janus to provide loans and investments in their electricity sector.

The FIB Programme will include a “borrowing project” which aims to be executed by the national electric utility and other financial institutions. This will enable the BBC to borrow funds from the national electric utility at an affordable cost.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL (V1.2) PAGE 4

## Programme Components

The UKIJ Programme is structured in a way that it not only addresses all the key risks and barriers passed during the feasibility study but also ensures that the programme is sustainable and capable of delivering the intended energy outcomes in the BRIFCs. The below figure outlines the key components of the programme.

![Figure 1: Structure of the UKIJ Programme](image_url)

### Components

| Component | Description |
|-----------|-------------|
| 1.1 Magnesium Iodide | Magnesium Iodide is used to improve the efficiency of combustion in internal combustion engines and diesel engines. |
| 1.2 Hydrogen Fuel Cells | Hydrogen fuel cells are used to power electric vehicles and other applications that require clean energy. |
| 1.3 Renewable Energy Sources | The programme includes various renewable energy sources such as solar, wind, and hydroelectric power. |

### Objectives

The Programme through Component 1 establishes and promotes UKIJ as a core element of each Executing Entity’s national energy strategy. It aims to bring about a significant reduction in carbon emissions by promoting the use of clean energy technologies and renewable resources in the BRIFCs.

Each programme component is broken down into subcomponents, which themselves comprise several activities that contribute towards achieving the overall objectives of the Programme.

### Programme Benefits

The UKIJ Programme achieves significant climate-related benefits:

- **Reduction in GHG Emissions**: The Programme is designed to avoid a cumulative total of 500,000 tonnes of CO2 equivalent emissions by 2030. This includes a reduction of 100,000 tonnes of CO2 equivalent and the CDM’s commitment to avoid approximately USD 42 per tCO2e avoided.
- **Increased Resilience Against Heat Stress**: The Programme is designed to provide energy efficient solutions that can withstand extreme weather conditions.
- **Decreased Burden on Utilities and Increased Renewable Energy Generation**: Energy efficiency measures will reduce the burden on existing utilities, while renewable energy generation will increase the overall capacity of clean energy in the region. This includes a targeted increase in the contribution of clean energy to the energy grids.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL (V1.2) PAGE 1

## A

The deployment of technologies like solar photovoltaics systems, solar water heating and possibly wind and combined energy, renewable or the country-specific, climate and natural resource inventory.

Encouraging the adoption of clean energy technologies through capacity building, knowledge sharing and financial incentives to promote opportunities for local businesses and communities. This will be achieved through:

- **Capacity Building and Knowledge Sharing**: A core outcome of the Programme is the enhancement of technical expertise and regulatory frameworks in each participating country. The technical training programme will include a range of activities such as workshops, seminars and field visits.

- **Building Up of Infrastructure**: By developing investment and establishing favourable conditions for private and international financing. The Programme aims to capitalize on existing resources for the energy sector infrastructure development and to promote regional cooperation among countries in the region.

- **Gender and Social Inclusion**: The Programme places an emphasis on ensuring that the benefits of this energy transition are shared equitably across all segments of society, particularly women and youth. The Programme aims to include a focus on gender equality and social inclusion (GESI), the Programme will enhance gender-neutral policies, governance and participation. Through this, it will ensure that women and marginalized groups have equal access to opportunities and benefits from the original actions focused on the marginalized beneficiaries.

#### B.1.1 Baseline, ambition, and targets

- **Global emissions**: Net-zero by 2050.
- **GHG emissions**: Net-zero by 2040.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL (V.1.2) PAGE 7

## B.1.4 Emission reduction targets

### Baseline

Bangladesh has estimated baseline targets as well as incremental emission reduction targets for different sectors, including the energy sector and the waste sector.

| Baseline | National Target |
|----------|-----------------|
| **Emissions (MtCO2e)** | **Reduction of CO2 emissions by 15% from BAU by 2030** |
| **Energy Sector** | **18% increase in energy efficiency, 20% share of renewable energy in the total electricity mix by 2030** |

### The Contribution to SDGs

The Government of Bangladesh has committed to achieving the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) through various initiatives. These include:

- Increasing access to clean and affordable energy.
- Improving energy efficiency.
- Promoting the use of renewable energy sources.

## B.1.5 Associated current and projected emissions from the sector without the program

### The Second Nationally Determined Contribution (NDC)

Bangladesh submitted its second NDC in 2020 for the energy sector to the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC). This includes:

- A commitment to reduce greenhouse gas emissions by 15% from BAU by 2030.
- An additional reduction of 5% through international support mechanisms.

### Emissions Profile

Emissions from Bangladesh are attributed to key sectors, energy and transport. The Government has committed to reducing emissions in these areas as part of its NDC.

## Results

### Emission reductions achieved so far

By 2021, the country had reduced emissions by 5% from the 2017 projected baseline with BAU assumptions.

## B.1.14: The Caribbean region, including Barbados, Belize, and Jamaica, is already facing, and will continue to face, significant impacts from climate change. The rise in temperature in the Caribbean is expected to increase severe weather events such as hurricanes, droughts, and floods. These changes are likely to have significant impacts on agriculture, water resources, and coastal infrastructure. The region's vulnerability is exacerbated by limited capacity to support adaptation and resilience against these impacts.

## Table 4: Historical and projected climate change impacts for the DMOs

### Jamaica

The average temperature in Jamaica has increased by approximately 1.5°C per decade, largely influenced by the El Niño Southern Oscillation, which significantly affects rainfall patterns. The country is experiencing more frequent and intense hurricanes, droughts, floods, and landslides. Notably, as evidenced by severe damage from Hurricanes with an El-Niño event in 2016.

### Projections

Modeling conducted under the GCF Readiness Program revealed that the World Wide Climate Change Initiative (WICC) projects a temperature increase of up to 3°C by the end of this century. This will have significant impacts on agriculture, water resources, and coastal infrastructure. The region's vulnerability is exacerbated by limited capacity to support adaptation and resilience against these impacts.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL

## B

The United States has a crucial role to play in global climate change mitigation and adaptation efforts. The U.S. can lead by example through ambitious domestic action that reduces greenhouse gas emissions, enhances resilience to the impacts of climate change, and supports international efforts to address this global challenge.

There are several key areas where the U.S. can make significant progress:

- **Carbon Pricing**: Implementing a carbon price can drive innovation and investment in low-carbon technologies.
- **Renewable Energy**: Expanding renewable energy sources such as wind and solar power.
- **Energy Efficiency**: Promoting energy efficiency in buildings, industry, and transportation.

### Figure 8: Projected GDP Changes in Average Annual Temperature

![Figure 8](image_url)

### Figure 9: Impacts for Heat Wave Heat Risk

![Figure 9](image_url)

Source: Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change (IPCC) reports from various years.

## Methodology

At the outset of the World Bank's CMPN assessment, average and minimum temperatures are projected to increase by approximately 1.5°C and 2°C respectively over the next century. This is based on current emissions trajectories and assumes no additional mitigation efforts.

### Populations

Models underlying the CMPN are based on the World Bank's CMPN projections. These models assume that populations will grow according to UN population projections, with an average annual growth rate of 1.05% under the most optimistic scenario (SSP2-4.5) reaching as high as 3.86°C by 2100. Under a business-as-usual scenario (SSP5-8.5), temperatures could rise by as much as 7.9°C by 2100.

### Impacts

Under an optimistic scenario, the average temperature increase is projected to be around 3.6°C by 2100. This would result in significant impacts on human health and ecosystems, including increased frequency and intensity of heatwaves, droughts, and storms. The effects of these changes are expected to be most severe in regions with high population density and limited adaptive capacity.

### Adaptation

Adaptation measures can help reduce the impact of climate change. These include improving infrastructure resilience, enhancing early warning systems, and promoting sustainable land use practices. However, without significant mitigation efforts, the impacts of climate change will continue to pose a threat to human societies and ecosystems around the world.

### B.1.15
The RFF Programme aims to facilitate the adoption of renewable energy and energy efficient technologies, supporting countries to address the above-described newsworthiness by:
- (i) increasing access to affordable clean energy;
- (ii) reducing greenhouse gas emissions from deforestation and forest degradation, and land use change;
- (iii) increasing resilience against the mounting risk of heat stress in the Caribbean; and
- (iv) enhancing sustainable development through the deployment of renewable energy technologies. This overarching goal is achieved by deploying the source of electricity generation and peak shaving for the domestic sector.

### B.1.16
Small Island Developing States (SIDS) in the Caribbean face common challenges inherent to their geographical and environmental contexts. These include:
- high vulnerability to natural disasters;
- limited access to financial resources; and
- dependence on imported fossil fuels.

The RFF Programme will support SIDS with its implementation, leveraging regional cooperation and integration to enhance the impact of climate finance. The programme will be implemented through a combination of policy actions and capacity-building initiatives.

A summary of relevant baseline conditions and characteristics in the three participating SIDS is provided below:

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1.3 PAGE 11

## B

### Climate Action. The Enhanced Light & Power Company (ELPCO) seeks a loan of USD 50 million to finance an energy efficiency and renewable energy project in the Philippines.

The project will be implemented by ELPCO, a private utility company that provides electricity to over 300,000 households and businesses in the Philippines. The project aims to reduce greenhouse gas emissions by promoting energy efficiency and renewable energy projects.

### Project Components

1. **Energy Efficiency Program**
   - Implementation of energy efficiency measures such as lighting upgrades, HVAC improvements, and motor replacements.
   - Training and capacity building for ELPCO staff and customers on energy management practices.
   - Promotion of energy-efficient appliances and equipment through rebates and incentives.

2. **Renewable Energy Program**
   - Installation of rooftop solar photovoltaic (PV) systems on eligible buildings.
   - Development of small-scale renewable energy projects, such as mini-hydro or wind power installations.
   - Grid integration and management of renewable energy sources to ensure reliability and stability of the electricity supply.

### Project Benefits

- **Environmental Benefits**: Reduction in greenhouse gas emissions through energy efficiency and renewable energy adoption.
- **Economic Benefits**: Cost savings for customers due to reduced electricity consumption and lower energy bills.
- **Social Benefits**: Job creation and capacity building opportunities in the renewable energy sector.

### Project Implementation

The project will be implemented over a period of five years, with the following milestones:
- **Year 1**: Conduct feasibility studies, develop project designs, and prepare procurement plans.
- **Years 2-3**: Implement energy efficiency measures and install renewable energy systems.
- **Year 4**: Monitor and evaluate project performance and impact.
- **Year 5**: Complete final reporting and close out the project.

### Project Funding

The project will be funded through a combination of loan and grant financing. The GCF funding will cover USD 30 million, with the remaining USD 20 million provided by ELPCO and other sources.

### Project Co-Financing

ELPCO has secured co-financing from various sources, including:
- **Local Banks**: Providing additional loans to support the project.
- **International Financial Institutions (IFIs)**: Offering grants and technical assistance to enhance project implementation.
- **Private Sector Investors**: Contributing equity and debt financing to the project.

### Project Management

The project will be managed by a dedicated team within ELPCO, responsible for overall project coordination, implementation, and monitoring. The team will include experts in energy efficiency, renewable energy, and project management.

### Project Risks

Potential risks associated with the project include:
- **Technical Risks**: Challenges in implementing new technologies or systems.
- **Financial Risks**: Fluctuations in energy prices or changes in funding availability.
- **Regulatory Risks**: Changes in government policies or regulations affecting renewable energy and energy efficiency.

### Project Monitoring and Evaluation

The project will be monitored and evaluated through regular reporting, site visits, and performance assessments. Key indicators include:
- **Energy Savings**: Measured in kilowatt-hours (kWh) saved.
- **Emissions Reductions**: Quantified in tons of CO2 equivalent (tCO2e).
- **Customer Satisfaction**: Assessed through surveys and feedback mechanisms.

### Project Compliance

The project will comply with relevant environmental and social safeguards, including:
- **Environmental Impact Assessment (EIA)**: Conducted to identify potential impacts and mitigation measures.
- **Social Impact Management Plan (SIMP)**: Developed to address any social issues or concerns related to the project.

### Project Documentation

All project documents, including feasibility studies, designs, procurement plans, and reports, will be made available upon request. The project will also comply with relevant regulations and standards set by the GCF and other international financial institutions.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1.0 PAGE 18

## B

### Executive Summary

The American electricity sector includes the largest Public Service Company (PSC) and investor-owned utility (IOU) systems in the United States. These utilities provide electric service to over half of the population, just under 50% of the nation's electricity sales, and account for nearly one-third of all greenhouse gas emissions from the U.S. electric power sector. The sector has a long history of innovation and leadership in environmental stewardship, including efforts to reduce air pollution, water use, and waste. Utilities have also been leaders in developing renewable energy resources and energy efficiency programs.

### RMCAs recognize for program development

#### R 1.1.3

All three entities have expressed a strong interest in implementing KIB to enhance their investment in distributed energy (DE) and storage systems. This commitment is enhanced by their long-term strategic plans that include the deployment of DE resources, including solar PV, battery storage, and electric vehicles.

#### R 1.2.4

The RMCAs recognize the need for an IOU Pilot Program, supported by DOE and Carnegie, to advance the RMCs' ability to implement KIB. The RMCs also recognize that the ability to participate directly in the pilot program is limited due to the small size of their individual programs. The RMCs are committed to supporting the development of a larger-scale pilot program that will include multiple IOUs and municipalities. The RMCs also recognize the need for a coordinated approach to the implementation of KIB, including the development of a common framework for the implementation of KIB.

#### R 1.3.6

The planned pilot component of the IOU Program is designed to accelerate capacity building and technical assistance efforts in support of KIB implementation. The RMCs recognize that the success of the program will depend on the ability of the utilities to implement KIB effectively and efficiently. The RMCs also recognize the need for a coordinated approach to the implementation of KIB, including the development of a common framework for the implementation of KIB.

### Energy efficiency in the proposed programme

The proposed programme will focus on increasing energy efficiency in lighting, air conditioning systems, and other equipment used in schools, hospitals, and other public buildings. This will be achieved through:

- The installation of LED lighting and solar PV systems.
- The implementation of energy management systems.
- The promotion of energy efficient appliances and equipment.

### History of climate change and disaster risk management (DRM) work

The proposed programme builds on the history of climate change and DRM work that has been undertaken by the UNDP in the region. This includes:

- The development of national adaptation plans.
- The implementation of climate-resilient infrastructure projects.
- The provision of technical assistance to governments and communities.

### Climate-proofing

The proposed programme will also focus on climate-proofing infrastructure and enhancing energy efficiency in schools, hospitals, and other public buildings. This will be achieved through:

- The installation of LED lighting and solar PV systems.
- The implementation of energy management systems.
- The promotion of energy efficient appliances and equipment.

### Finance and targeted climate planning impacts those most vulnerable to climate change

The proposed programme aims to finance and target climate planning impacts those most vulnerable to climate change. This will be achieved through:

- The provision of technical assistance to governments and communities.
- The implementation of climate-resilient infrastructure projects.
- The promotion of energy efficient appliances and equipment.

### Goal

The goal of the proposed programme is to address a climate challenge through a sustainable impact in the BMCs. This will be achieved by:

- Increasing access to clean energy and energy efficient technologies.
- Enhancing the capacity of communities to adapt to climate change.
- Promoting the use of renewable energy sources.

### 8.2.3.4 The proposed RMI Programme aims to reduce GHG emissions from the energy sector and promote sustainable development

The proposed programme aims to reduce GHG emissions from the energy sector and promote sustainable development by:

- Increasing access to clean energy and energy efficient technologies.
- Enhancing the capacity of communities to adapt to climate change.
- Promoting the use of renewable energy sources.

### 8.2.5.
This program aims to enhance knowledge and capacity building for local communities to address climate change adaptation and mitigation needs. It will support the development of local action plans, community-based natural resource management programs, and sustainable livelihood initiatives. The program will also provide training and technical assistance to build local capacities in climate-smart agriculture, water management, and renewable energy.

### 8.2.6.
This program aims to be a long-term sustainable, scalable, and replicable solution to address the impact of climate change on vulnerable communities. It will focus on providing access to clean energy technologies and improving resilience to climate impacts. Goal: Enhance community resilience by:

- (a) ensuring robust market opportunities for renewable energy technologies;
- (b) strengthening local institutions' capacity to advance clean energy in particular countries; and
- (c) enhancing the technical capacity of Technology Development Providers (TDPs) to cater to the needs of the market.

### 8.2.7.
This program aims to support small-scale projects that address household, water, and sanitation needs. It will also address energy solutions and provide access to affordable finance, etc.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL V3.0 | PAGE 23

## Figure 15: SIB Programs Theory of Change

### Green Climate Fund (GCF)

- **Programme Name:** GCF
- **Description:** The Green Climate Fund is a global fund created to support the efforts of developing countries to respond to the challenge of climate change.
- **Objective:** To promote a low-emission, climate-resilient development pathway.

### SIB Programs

#### Programme 1: Renewable Energy

- **Name:** Renewable Energy
- **Description:** Promotes the adoption of renewable energy sources such as solar and wind power.
- **Objective:** Reduce greenhouse gas emissions by increasing the share of renewable energy in the energy mix.

#### Programme 2: Energy Efficiency

- **Name:** Energy Efficiency
- **Description:** Encourages the implementation of energy-efficient technologies and practices.
- **Objective:** Improve energy efficiency to reduce overall energy consumption and lower carbon footprint.

#### Programme 3: Climate-Smart Agriculture

- **Name:** Climate-Smart Agriculture
- **Description:** Supports agricultural practices that enhance productivity, increase resilience, and reduce greenhouse gas emissions.
- **Objective:** Enhance food security while mitigating climate change impacts on agriculture.

### Outputs

#### Renewable Energy

- **Output 1:** Installation of solar panels
- **Output 2:** Construction of wind farms
- **Output 3:** Development of hydroelectric power plants

#### Energy Efficiency

- **Output 1:** Implementation of LED lighting systems
- **Output 2:** Upgrading to energy-efficient appliances
- **Output 3:** Building retrofits for improved insulation and efficiency

#### Climate-Smart Agriculture

- **Output 1:** Adoption of climate-resilient crop varieties
- **Output 2:** Integration of agroforestry practices
- **Output 3:** Implementation of sustainable irrigation methods

### Outcomes

#### Renewable Energy

- **Outcome 1:** Increased share of renewable energy in the national energy mix
- **Outcome 2:** Reduced greenhouse gas emissions from the energy sector
- **Outcome 3:** Enhanced energy security through diversified energy sources

#### Energy Efficiency

- **Outcome 1:** Improved energy efficiency in households and businesses
- **Outcome 2:** Reduced overall energy consumption
- **Outcome 3:** Lowered carbon footprint of the economy

#### Climate-Smart Agriculture

- **Outcome 1:** Enhanced agricultural productivity and resilience to climate change
- **Outcome 2:** Increased food security for vulnerable populations
- **Outcome 3:** Reduced greenhouse gas emissions from agriculture

### Impact

#### Renewable Energy

- **Impact 1:** Mitigation of climate change through reduced carbon emissions
- **Impact 2:** Economic growth and job creation in the renewable energy sector
- **Impact 3:** Improved access to clean, affordable energy

#### Energy Efficiency

- **Impact 1:** Energy cost savings for households and businesses
- **Impact 2:** Reduced demand for fossil fuels, leading to lower emissions
- **Impact 3:** Enhanced competitiveness of industries through improved efficiency

#### Climate-Smart Agriculture

- **Impact 1:** Improved livelihoods and food security for farmers
- **Impact 2:** Enhanced ecosystem services and biodiversity
- **Impact 3:** Reduced vulnerability to climate-related disasters in rural communities

#### 9.2.4.5 To achieve the over-arching goal the Programme aims to deliver the following two main Outcomes:

**Outcome:** Reduced GHG emissions through widespread adoption of RE, EE and other DEI technologies.

The Programme is designed to promote reliance on lower emission technologies to meet the energy needs of the country. The Programme will support the development of a robust regulatory framework for renewable energy sector.

**Outcome:** Increased access to financing for clean energy investments, allowing utility companies, IPPs, EE and other DEI technologies to scale.

#### Outputs

##### 9.2.4.5.1 To achieve the two main Outcomes and one intermediate Outcome described above, the Programme will deliver the following Outputs:

1. **Output 1.1.1:** Strengthen institutional capacity and technical networks facilitating the adoption of RE, EE and other DEI technologies.

The Programme will support the partner countries and the key stakeholders to develop and implement policies, strategies and regulatory frameworks that promote renewable energy development and enhance energy efficiency. ## 4.2.1.1. Constitution of development HEIs and other OER institutions as a civil means of solidifying the growth of the green economy

### 4.2.1.1.1. Constitution of development HEIs and other OER institutions as a civil means of solidifying the growth of the green economy

The constitution of development HEIs and other OER institutions as a civil means of solidifying the growth of the green economy is a critical step in ensuring that the benefits of this initiative are fully realized. This section will discuss the importance of these institutions in promoting sustainable development and energy efficiency, as well as their role in fostering a culture of innovation and entrepreneurship.

### 4.2.1.1.2. The role of development HEIs and other OER institutions

Development HEIs and other OER institutions play a crucial role in the promotion of sustainable development and energy efficiency. They are responsible for providing education and training to students, as well as conducting research and developing new technologies that can help to reduce greenhouse gas emissions and promote sustainable practices.

### 4.2.1.1.3. The importance of these institutions in promoting sustainable development

The role of development HEIs and other OER institutions is essential in promoting sustainable development. They are responsible for providing education and training to students, as well as conducting research and developing new technologies that can help to reduce greenhouse gas emissions and promote sustainable practices.

# GREAT CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL v1.3 PAGE 8

## Understand legal and regulatory framework

- **SMO:** need closer look at regulatory landscape on various jurisdictions.
- **Limited customer awareness and support:** Consumers often lack awareness of the benefits of the technology.
- **Limited availability of TSPs providing end-to-end solutions:** While there are TSPs with the necessary components, few offer complete solutions.
  - **Recommendation:** Investing in bringing standardized high-quality solutions to the market can provide a strong foundation for new applications and a lack of expensive licensing problems.
- **Risk adverse stakeholders:** Both small electric customers and larger institutions are reluctant to adopt new technology solutions. Risk aversion is often exacerbated by lack of understanding and high initial costs.
  - **Recommendation:** Need a mix of government support and incentives to reduce the risk.
- **Capacity constraints and model understanding:** Regulatory authorities lack capacity to understand and evaluate new technologies, which can lead to delays in approvals.
  - **Recommendation:** Provide training and resources to regulatory bodies.
- **Limited locally-relevant business models:** High cost of financing and lack of local expertise are major challenges for companies to invest in financial aspects of distributed RE, EE, and storage.
  - **Recommendation:** Need more support from government agencies to help develop these business models.
- **Limited distribution RE and EE acceptance:** A possible explanation is the lack of understanding of the benefits of distributed energy resources (DERs).
  - **Recommendation:** More education and outreach efforts are needed.

### B.2.14 The activities in this section in Section B.3 have been designed to address the following barriers to implementing the need identified that these barriers impede adoption of RE and EE technologies in developing countries.
- **Limited understanding of DER benefits:** Lack of awareness about the advantages of DERs.
  - **Recommendation:** Need more education and outreach efforts to increase understanding.

## Summary

### Key Recommendations
1. **Regulatory Support:**
   - Regulators need to be more supportive of DER adoption.
   - Policies should encourage DER deployment.

2. **Customer Awareness:**
   - Increase consumer awareness about the benefits of DERs.
   - Provide incentives for early adopters.

3. **Standardization and Availability:**
   - Promote standardization in TSP offerings.
   - Encourage more TSPs to offer end-to-end solutions.

4. **Risk Mitigation:**
   - Government support and incentives can reduce risk aversion.
   - Policies should address the high initial costs of DER adoption.

5. **Capacity Building:**
   - Provide training and resources for regulatory authorities.
   - Increase understanding of DER benefits among regulators.

6. **Business Model Development:**
   - Support local expertise in developing business models.
   - Encourage investment in financial aspects of distributed RE, EE, and storage.

7. **Education and Outreach:**
   - More education is needed to increase understanding of DER benefits.
   - Outreach efforts should target both consumers and regulators.

### Next Steps
- Develop a comprehensive plan for addressing these barriers.
- Engage with stakeholders to implement the recommendations.
- Monitor progress and adjust strategies as needed.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal v.1.3 (Page 29)

## Figure 14: Summary of GCF programs rationale

### 1. Enhancement of the Energy Sector in SIDS Countries
- **Rationale**: The energy sector is a key driver for economic growth and development.
- **Objectives**:
  - Increase access to affordable, reliable, sustainable, and modern energy services.
  - Promote renewable energy adoption.

### 2. National Adaptation Planning (NAP)
- **Rationale**: Climate change impacts are increasing the need for adaptation planning.
- **Objectives**:
  - Integrate climate risk management into national development planning.
  - Enhance resilience to climate change.

### 3. Financial Accessibility and Affordability
- **Rationale**: Access to finance is a critical barrier to achieving GCF objectives.
- **Objectives**:
  - Improve access to affordable financing for climate action.
  - Promote innovative financial instruments.

### 4. Implementation of Nationally Determined Contributions (NDCs)
- **Rationale**: NDCs are the foundation for countries' climate commitments under the Paris Agreement.
- **Objectives**:
  - Support implementation of NDCs.
  - Enhance transparency and reporting on progress.

## Summary: Strategic Objectives and Approaches

### 1. Enhancing Energy Efficiency and Renewable Energy
- **Objective**: Increase access to modern energy services.
- **Approach**: Promote renewable energy technologies and energy efficiency measures.

### 2. Strengthening National Adaptation Planning for Resilience
- **Objective**: Integrate climate risk management into national planning.
- **Approach**: Support NAP processes and enhance resilience to climate change impacts.

### 3. Improving Financial Accessibility and Affordability
- **Objective**: Enhance access to affordable financing.
- **Approach**: Promote innovative financial instruments and improve access to climate finance.

### 4. Supporting Implementation of Nationally Determined Contributions (NDCs)
- **Objective**: Support NDC implementation.
- **Approach**: Provide technical assistance and enhance transparency reporting.

### 5. Regional Learning and Knowledge Sharing Platform
- **Objective**: Facilitate regional cooperation on climate action.
- **Approach**: Establish a regional platform for knowledge sharing and collaboration.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal

## B. Programme Approach

### 1. The GCF Programme will include a “lab-based project” to each of the three countries to be executed by the respective national universities.

Each programme module is a series of activities that capture, validate and verify greenhouse gas emissions from land use changes and other human activities. The programme will also include capacity-building workshops for local stakeholders with technical assistance. The goal is to develop the country’s own carbon management system, which will allow them to assess their own carbon footprint and implement mitigation measures.

### 2. The GCF Programme will also include a “lab-based project” to each of the three countries to be executed by the respective national universities.

Each programme module is a series of activities that capture, validate and verify greenhouse gas emissions from land use changes and other human activities. The programme will also include capacity-building workshops for local stakeholders with technical assistance. The goal is to develop the country’s own carbon management system, which will allow them to assess their own carbon footprint and implement mitigation measures.

The GCF and its programme structure is designed to support the implementation of renewable energy projects, carbon management systems, and other climate-related activities.

## Figure 18: Programme governance arrangements

### Components of the SUP Programme

#### Component 1: Establish and promote SUP as a new instrument of electric utilities' business models

The Programme through Component 1 aims to establish and promote SUP as a new instrument of electric utilities' business models. The Programme will provide technical assistance in the development of SUP policies, guidelines, and regulations. It will also support the implementation of SUP projects by providing financial resources and capacity building.

## Programme description

### 0.3 The SUP Program is designed to address key barriers that are preventing the adoption of sustainable energy solutions in the BMCs. These include lack of access to finance, inadequate policy and regulatory frameworks, and limited technical expertise. The programme will focus on addressing these barriers through a combination of technical assistance, capacity building, and financial support.

### Objectives

1. **Objective 1:** Increase access to affordable and sustainable energy solutions for households in the BMCs.
2. **Objective 2:** Promote the adoption of renewable energy technologies by small and medium-sized enterprises (SMEs) in the BMCs.
3. **Objective 3:** Enhance the capacity of local governments to develop and implement policies that support sustainable energy development.

### Components

1. **Component 1: Establish and promote SUP as a new instrument of electric utilities' business models**
   - The Programme through Component 1 aims to establish and promote SUP as a new instrument of electric utilities' business models.
   - The Programme will provide technical assistance in the development of SUP policies, guidelines, and regulations.
   - It will also support the implementation of SUP projects by providing financial resources and capacity building.

2. **Component 2: Enhance access to sustainable energy solutions for households**
   - This component aims to increase access to affordable and sustainable energy solutions for households in the BMCs.
   - The Programme will provide technical assistance, capacity building, and financial support to households to adopt renewable energy technologies.

3. **Component 3: Promote adoption of renewable energy technologies by SMEs**
   - This component aims to promote the adoption of renewable energy technologies by small and medium-sized enterprises (SMEs) in the BMCs.
   - The Programme will provide technical assistance, capacity building, and financial support to SMEs to adopt renewable energy technologies.

4. **Component 4: Enhance capacity of local governments**
   - This component aims to enhance the capacity of local governments to develop and implement policies that support sustainable energy development.
   - The Programme will provide technical assistance, capacity building, and financial support to local governments to develop and implement sustainable energy policies.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1.1 PAGE 33

## Activity 1.2 Address to strengthen policy frameworks needs to strengthen projects and initiatives (including the development of a national climate change policy)

The GCF Readiness Program is designed to support developing countries in their efforts to prepare for, and benefit from, climate change adaptation and mitigation. This includes strengthening national institutions and policies, enhancing data collection and monitoring systems, and building capacity to access and implement climate finance. The program aims to ensure that projects are aligned with the objectives of the Paris Agreement and contribute to the global effort to limit temperature increases.

### Output 1.2.1 Enhanced capacity of countries to increase investments in distributed RE, EE and other DERs through the NDB Model

The National Development Bank (NDB) model is designed to support countries in their efforts to increase investments in renewable energy (RE), energy efficiency (EE), and other distributed energy resources (DERs). The model aims to enhance the capacity of national development banks to provide financing for projects that contribute to climate change mitigation and adaptation. This includes providing technical assistance, capacity building, and access to financing.

### Output 1.2.2 Enhanced capacity of countries to increase investments in distributed RE, EE and other DERs through the NDB Model

The National Development Bank (NDB) model is designed to support countries in their efforts to increase investments in renewable energy (RE), energy efficiency (EE), and other distributed energy resources (DERs). The model aims to enhance the capacity of national development banks to provide financing for projects that contribute to climate change mitigation and adaptation. This includes providing technical assistance, capacity building, and access to financing.

## Activity 1.3 Enhance awareness of RE-EE initiatives and potential benefits under the activity

### Output 1.3.1 Enhanced public awareness of RE-EE initiatives and potential benefits

Under this activity, the GCF Readiness Program will support countries in their efforts to enhance public awareness of renewable energy (RE) and energy efficiency (EE) initiatives and their potential benefits. This includes developing communication strategies, conducting public outreach campaigns, and providing information on the benefits of RE and EE projects.

### Output 1.3.2 Enhanced capacity of countries to increase investments in distributed RE, EE and other DERs through the NDB Model

Under this activity, the GCF Readiness Program will support countries in their efforts to enhance public awareness of renewable energy (RE) and energy efficiency (EE) initiatives and their potential benefits. This includes developing communication strategies, conducting public outreach campaigns, and providing information on the benefits of RE and EE projects.

Under this activity, the GCF Readiness Program will also provide technical assistance to countries in their efforts to develop and implement policies and regulations that support the deployment of renewable energy (RE) and energy efficiency (EE) technologies. This includes providing policy advice, conducting policy dialogues, and supporting the development of regulatory frameworks.

To help countries develop and fund projects under (Component 2) as well as faster long-term climate energy market transformation.

## Executing Entity: GCF via the TA Execution Teams

### Source of funds: GCF grant financing

#### Activity 5.2.2 Promote new inclusive and fast RE market and associated services
Through its Technical Assistance Programme (TAP), the GCF TA Execution Team will promote the development of a sustainable, inclusive, and competitive RE market by providing technical assistance to countries to develop and implement policies that support the growth of RE markets. This includes promoting the use of innovative technologies and business models for RE projects, as well as supporting the development of new RE markets.

**Executing Entity:** GCF via the TA Execution Teams

**Source of funds:** GCF grant financing

#### Activity 5.2.3 Promote customers with households to identify and deploy distributed RE systems
The GCF TA Execution Team will promote the deployment of distributed RE systems by providing technical assistance to countries to develop policies that support the use of distributed RE systems. This includes promoting the use of innovative technologies and business models for distributed RE systems, as well as supporting the development of new distributed RE markets.

**Executing Entity:** GCF via the TA Execution Teams

**Source of funds:** GCF grant financing

#### Activity 5.2.4 Promote the deployment of RE technologies
The GCF TA Execution Team will promote the deployment of RE technologies by providing technical assistance to countries to develop policies that support the use of RE technologies. This includes promoting the use of innovative technologies and business models for RE projects, as well as supporting the development of new RE markets.

**Executing Entity:** GCF via the TA Execution Teams

**Source of funds:** GCF grant financing

## Outcome 5: Increased deployment of RE and other clean technologies
To increase the deployment of RE and other clean technologies, the GCF TA Execution Team will provide technical assistance to countries to develop policies that support the use of RE technologies. This includes promoting the use of innovative technologies and business models for RE projects, as well as supporting the development of new RE markets.

**Executing Entity:** GCF via the TA Execution Teams

**Source of funds:** GCF grant financing

## B

### Activity 1.3.7 Facilitate knowledge exchange among the participating BMUs

Under this activity, the GCF TA Execution Team will work with an external service provider and national counterparts to facilitate knowledge exchange among the participating BMUs. This includes organizing workshops, webinars, and other events to share best practices on climate finance management using both online platforms and in-person meetings.

**Executing Entity:** GCF via the TA Execution Team

#### Summary of activities: GCF grant management

### Activity 1.3.2 Enhance technical and other stakeholders' capacities to implement the GCF Readiness Programme

This activity will focus on building the capacity of national designated authorities (NDAs) and accredited entities to effectively manage their climate finance portfolios. This includes training workshops, e-learning courses, and one-on-one mentoring sessions.

**Executing Entity:** GCF via the TA Execution Team

#### Summary of activities: GCF grant management

### Activity 1.3.5 Train qualified (technological) service providers - notably TSPs - to support BMUs in implementing their climate finance portfolios

This activity will focus on training qualified service providers, particularly technology-focused providers (TSPs), to support the BMUs in implementing their climate finance portfolios. The training will cover areas such as project preparation, procurement, and financial management.

**Executing Entity:** GCF via the TA Execution Team

#### Summary of activities: GCF grant management

### Activity 1.3: Support international exchange with other developing countries, led by BMU and GIZ

**Objective:** To support the implementation of the GCF grant in MACS through regional workshops.

### Closing Entity: GCF via its Accreditation Team

- **Outcome:** Enhanced capacity building and knowledge sharing among stakeholders.

### Component 2: Facilitate use of environmentally distributed RE, EE and other DERs by implementing the SDG model at state and local levels through integrated planning.

**Output 2.1:** Development of a comprehensive approach to facilitate the use of renewable energy (RE), energy efficiency (EE) and distributed renewable energy systems (DERs).

- **Outcome:** Enhanced understanding of the benefits of using RE, EE and DERs in achieving SDGs.

### Closing Entity: GCF via its Accreditation Team

### Output 2.1: Implementation of distributed RE, EE and other DER technologies, as well as complementary measures to enhance energy efficiency and reduce greenhouse gas emissions in public sector entities.

- **Outcome:** Increased use of renewable energy sources.
- **Activity:** Development of integrated approaches that support the adoption of renewable energy projects at state and local levels.

## Transmitted Technologies

The following technologies have been identified for inclusion in the 1.5 Phase program, with a focus on LED lighting, efficient air conditioning systems, solar rooftop PV (both or individual storage), and energy-efficient appliances. These technologies are highlighted in the other chapters; the overall objective is to identify countries that can benefit from these technologies.

### 8.3.10
There are however unmet needs across the region that may be included in a package service offering for the countries with the least developed technologies. This includes solar rooftop, efficient air conditioning systems, and energy-efficient appliances. These technologies have been identified as having high potential to reduce greenhouse gas emissions.

|       | The proposal is to install solar panels on the rooftop of existing buildings and set up a small wind turbine in an open area. This will generate clean electricity for use within the campus, reducing reliance on grid electricity. |
|       | **Who benefits:** Students, faculty, and staff who utilize the campus facilities. |
|       | **When completed by:** End of 2030. |
|       | **Funding required:** $500,000. |
|       | **Technology used:** Solar panels (Photovoltaic), small wind turbine. |
|       | **Implementation steps:** Conduct a feasibility study, obtain necessary permits, install solar panels and wind turbine, connect to the campus grid, monitor and maintain the system. |

| Item | Description |
|------|-------------|
| **1.2** | **Heating and Cooling** |
|       | The proposal is to replace existing HVAC systems with energy-efficient ones. This will reduce energy consumption for heating and cooling, making the campus more sustainable. |
|       | **Who benefits:** Students, faculty, and staff who utilize the campus facilities. |
|       | **When completed by:** End of 2031. |
|       | **Funding required:** $450,000. |
|       | **Technology used:** Energy-efficient HVAC systems. |
|       | **Implementation steps:** Conduct a feasibility study, obtain necessary permits, replace existing HVAC systems, connect to the campus grid, monitor and maintain the system. |

| Item | Description |
|------|-------------|
| **1.3** | **Lighting Upgrade** |
|       | The proposal is to upgrade all lighting fixtures on campus to LED lights. This will significantly reduce energy consumption and maintenance costs. |
|       | **Who benefits:** Students, faculty, and staff who utilize the campus facilities. |
|       | **When completed by:** End of 2031. |
|       | **Funding required:** $350,000. |
|       | **Technology used:** LED lighting fixtures. |
|       | **Implementation steps:** Conduct a feasibility study, obtain necessary permits, replace existing lighting fixtures with LEDs, monitor and maintain the system. |

# GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING-PROPOSAL V.3.0 | PAGE 45

![Figure 15: Application screening process flow](image-url)

- **Green Climate Fund**
- **Application Screening Process Flow**

## Key

- **Red**: Initial Submission
- **Blue**: Eligibility Check
- **Yellow**: Full Proposal Submission
- **Green**: Project Approval
- **Gray**: Rejected Applications

## Application Screening Process Flow

1. **Initial Submission** (Red)
   - Applicants submit their initial proposals.

2. **Eligibility Check** (Blue)
   - GCF conducts an eligibility check on the submitted proposals.
     - If eligible, proceed to Full Proposal Submission.
     - If not eligible, the application is rejected.

3. **Full Proposal Submission** (Yellow)
   - Eligible applicants submit detailed full proposals.

4. **Project Approval** (Green)
   - GCF reviews and approves projects that meet criteria.
     - Approved projects proceed to funding.
     - Unapproved projects may be resubmitted or rejected.

5. **Rejected Applications** (Gray)
   - Applications that do not pass eligibility checks or full proposal reviews are rejected.

- Source: GCF

# The CDB

As the host country entity, the CDB will provide financing, financial oversight, overall management and administrative support to the GCF and CDB. It will also manage the blended finance (green energy fund) and other related activities.

## Sustainability Unit (SU)

The SU is the focal point for the Green Climate Fund (GCF). It will be responsible for supporting all programme areas of the CDB in relation to climate change, including the management of the GCF. The SU will also provide technical support to the Board of Directors and the Executive Committee.

## Board: The Board of Directors of the Caribbean Development Bank

The ultimate decision-making body of the CDB is its Board of Directors. The Board is responsible for approving the Bank's programme of operations, including loans, guarantees and other financial arrangements. The Board also oversees the management of the Bank to ensure that it achieves its objectives.

### Management

Management includes the President as well as Vice Presidents, Departmental Directors and Managers. The management team is responsible for implementing the decisions of the Board of Directors and ensuring that the Bank operates in accordance with the Bank's programme of operations.

Other related areas within CDB that will support the programme through oversight on Office of Independent Evaluation: The CDB Office of Independent Evaluation will be involved in monitoring, evaluating and reporting on the effectiveness of the programme.

### Procurement Policy Unit (PPU)

The Procurement Policy Unit (PPU) is tasked with ensuring that all procurement activities are conducted in accordance with the Bank's Procurement Manual. The PPU also includes a Client Service Unit that provides support to the various departments and divisions within the Bank.

### Government

Governments, departments and agencies are involved in shaping energy policy and overseeing energy sector performance. Departments such as the Ministry of Finance, Ministry of Energy and Public Utilities are responsible for implementing policies related to energy efficiency and renewable energy.

The success of a bottom-up RESI market is grounded in the government's ability to create an enabling environment that supports clean energy development.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal v.1.1

## B

Government intervention to eliminate barriers, and underlying business models may be necessary to ensure that energy efficiency and decarbonization policies are implemented.

### Revenue Government Missions

- **Ministry of Business**
- **Ministry of Public Services - Energy**
- **Ministry of Economic Development**

### NDAs

National Development Authorities are government institutions that serve as the operative arm of the government in implementing national development plans. The NDAs are responsible for ensuring the country's readiness for achieving the goals set out by the government.

The respective NDAs act through various programmatic initiatives (e.g., the Green Climate Fund - GCF) and other programs to support the business sector with sustainable energy solutions. The NDAs also work closely with the Ministry of Finance, the Ministry of Business, and other relevant ministries to ensure that the country's development goals are met.

### GOV-NDA

- **Ministry of Finance**
  - **After Tax Incentives**
  - **Public Investment Funds**
- **Ministry of Business**

### Regulator

Never explicitly modeled such as the UK, could use new tax revenue for utilities. To integrate with existing regulatory frameworks, the regulator would need to ensure that the utility companies are compliant with the regulations and standards set by the government.

### Energy Providers

- **Electricity Companies**
  - **Public Commission**
  - **Utility Commission**

### Utility (Exiting Entities)

The exiting entities will be required to exit from the UKI programs who are already engaged with GCF and other RE funds. It is a voluntary choice for existing entities to continue their engagement in the energy transition process. As an act, utilities may also engage in new business models that support the government's objectives.

Under the UKI governance structure, the Exiting Entities will exit as far as possible from the UKI programs and will be required to comply with the regulations set out by the government. The exiting entities will be included in the existing entities list.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal V.1.0 PAGE 8

## Approach

The charges to the billing system for utility companies will be reduced by 50% through a combination of a reduction in the number of calls and a reduction in the time taken to resolve each call.

### Actions

- Executing Entire Set of Tasks: All tasks will be executed with the highest level of professionalism, integrity, and efficiency.
- Reducing Call Volume: The volume of calls received by the billing system will be reduced by 50% through a combination of improved customer service (inbound) and improved internal processes (outbound).
- Reducing Time to Resolve Calls: The time taken to resolve each call will be reduced by 50% through a combination of improved customer service (inbound) and improved internal processes (outbound).

### Participating Entities

- **Technology Provider**: TSP
- **Banks**: Rentech Limited, Electra
- **Jamaica Public Service Company Limited**

## Technology Service Provider

The Technology Service Provider (TSP) will be responsible for providing services to the utility companies. The TSP will provide a range of services including:

- Providing a billing system that is integrated with the utility company's existing systems.
- Providing training and support to the utility companies' staff.
- Providing ongoing maintenance and support to ensure the system remains operational.

The TSP will also be responsible for ensuring that the billing system meets the needs of the utility companies. The TSP will work closely with the utility companies to ensure that the system is tailored to their specific requirements.

### Other Approved Stakeholders

- **CCREEE**: Has been involved with the development of the I&I program and has provided technical assistance to the utility companies.
- **CARILEC**: Provides language translation services to help the IAC achieve its objectives.
- **CAREC**: Provides training and capacity building in energy efficiency for the utility companies.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL

## CARBON CAPTURE USE OF FUNDS (CCUOF)

### Business Association

Association for Barbados Industrial and Development Corporation (ABIDC) Chamber of Commerce and Industry (BCCI) and the Barbados Private Sector Association (BPSA). These organizations will be managed by the respective energy associations. It is recommended that these organizations be included in the IED program.

### Customers

The customers of the ABIDC represent a significant portion of all BCCI members, thereby the top generation and energy consumers. These include hotels, banks, retail outlets, and other small to medium-sized enterprises (SMEs). The SMEs are the most important and largest customers for this program.

### TA Execution Team

The TA Execution Team will be established within the ABIDC and will execute all of the TA activities under the programs led by experienced managers. This team will be supported by the National Energy Efficiency Agency (NEEA) and the Barbados Light & Power Company (BL&P). The TA Execution Team will be responsible for the implementation of the IED program.

### TA Implementation Support Consultant

The TA Implementation Support Consultant will provide continued technical assistance to the TA Execution Team. This consultant will be supported by the NEEA and BL&P. The TA Implementation Support Consultant will work closely with the TA Execution Team to ensure that all activities are implemented effectively and efficiently.

### Network Energy Consumers

Global Network of Energy Consumers (GNEC) will be approached to participate in the program. This network will provide a platform for the exchange of best practices, experiences, and lessons learned among its members. The GNEC will also support the implementation of the IED program.

### 8.5.4. Grant financing

The programme will further enhance existing arrangements with development partners to strengthen the evidence base for climate-smart investments in the energy sector. This includes exploring options such as blended finance, which can leverage private capital and reduce the need for public funding. The programme will also explore innovative approaches to climate change adaptation and resilience building. This will involve generating an evidence base for decision-making on climate change adaptation and resilience building.

### 8.5.5. The DOF

The DOF is uniquely suited to provide the above-mentioned types of financing for the US programme. It has a long history of providing technical assistance to developing countries, including through the Global Environment Facility (GEF) window, which can be used to leverage private sector investment in climate change adaptation and mitigation projects. The DOF also has experience working with the World Bank and other international financial institutions to support climate change adaptation and mitigation projects.

### 8.5.6. Co-Financing

The DOF expects to provide co-financing amounting to about USD 10.2 million as part of the programme's overall financing arrangements.

### 8.5.7. Early financing

These works, especially the I part, will be financed by the US government.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1.1 PAGE B

## Reduced risks and opportunities and engage in strengthening the sustainable energy transition

The GCF is a key financing mechanism to support developing countries in achieving their climate change mitigation and adaptation goals. By doing so, the programme aims to build significant knowledge and delivery benefits for both public and private sector stakeholders. The programme will focus on three main segments, each as residual customers, who actively engage to secure affordable financing for each segment.

### End beneficiaries

The FA and its implementing partners of this programme pays a significant focus on end-beneficiaries. The programme aims to ensure that the benefits of the programme are shared with the end-beneficiaries through the following means:

- **Technology transfer**: The programme will support the development and deployment of new technologies and off-grid services. This includes the potential uptake of EdTech solutions over the long-term.
- **Capacity building**: The programme aims to build capacity among end-beneficiaries, through technical and commercial training and awareness-raising activities. While the programme focuses on end-beneficiaries, it is essential to be aware of the benefits of the applied technologies, such as renewable energy sources and energy efficiency measures.
- **Knowledge sharing**: The programme will also focus on knowledge-sharing initiatives that support the uptake of new technologies by end-beneficiaries. This includes the development of knowledge-sharing platforms and networks to facilitate the exchange of information and best practices among end-beneficiaries.

Overall, the programme aims to generate a lasting demonstration effect for the adoption of sustainable energy solutions. The programme will also focus on the development of many provider technologies and energy efficiency applications are expected to decrease over time. After the programme is implemented, it is expected that the demand for these services will increase significantly. This includes the uptake of new technologies by end-beneficiaries and the development of new markets.

## Technology Development

| Context | Activities and Benefits |
|---------|------------------------|
| **Context** | Support activities that promote the use of climate-friendly technologies, including those that enhance the resilience of communities to the adverse effects of climate change. |
| **Objective 1.1: Climate Technology Development and Deployment** | Promote the development and deployment of climate technology solutions in developing countries with the aim of enhancing their capacity to mitigate greenhouse gas emissions and adapt to climate change. |
| **Objective 2.1: Knowledge Exchange and Capacity Building** | Support knowledge exchange and capacity building activities that enhance the ability of developing countries to participate effectively in global climate change negotiations, including through South-South cooperation. |
| **Objective 3.1: Technology Needs Assessment** | Conduct technology needs assessments to identify priority areas for technology development and deployment, and to support the development of national technology strategies. |

## Technology Development

- Support research and development activities that lead to the creation of new technologies or improvements in existing ones.
- Facilitate the transfer of technology between developed and developing countries through partnerships with international organizations, such as UNFCCC, UNDP, and others.
- Promote the use of climate-friendly technologies in various sectors, including energy, transport, agriculture, and industry.

## Technology Needs Assessment

- Conduct comprehensive assessments to identify priority areas for technology development and deployment.
- Support the development of national technology strategies that align with the objectives of the Paris Agreement.
- Facilitate the exchange of information on technology needs and gaps between developed and developing countries through regional and global networks.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal V.1.0

## Page 44

### Reference Number: GCF/XX/YY

#### Project Title: Renewable Energy Development

---

## Interest Bearers

The project aims to benefit from individual or collective efforts, although not directly participating in the programme. It demands a significant investment in the primary emission technologies and is likely to delay its impact due to the need for infrastructure development. The project will be implemented by the government, considering local development and demand-driven inclusion. The project will also benefit from the support of international organizations.

### 0.1 Project Summary

The project aims to reduce greenhouse gas emissions through the implementation of renewable energy systems and energy efficiency measures. The project will focus on developing and implementing new technologies for renewable energy generation and storage, as well as promoting energy conservation practices among households and businesses.

#### a. Objectives
- To achieve at least 50% reduction in greenhouse gas emissions by 2030.
- To promote the use of renewable energy sources such as solar, wind, and hydro power.
- To develop and implement new technologies for energy storage and distribution.
- To encourage the adoption of energy-efficient practices among households and businesses.

#### b. Costs
The programme also aims to reduce greenhouse gas emissions by 20% within five years. The project will be implemented in two phases over a period of ten years, with an initial investment of $10 million. The total cost of the project is estimated at $50 million, with funding provided by the Green Climate Fund and other international organizations.

### 0.2.1 Project Implementation

The project will be implemented through the following activities:

- Development of renewable energy systems
- Promotion of energy efficiency measures
- Training and capacity building for local communities
- Monitoring and evaluation of project progress

#### a. Benefits
- The project will create jobs in the renewable energy sector.
- The project will reduce greenhouse gas emissions by 20% within five years.
- The project will promote the use of renewable energy sources.

#### b. Costs
The total cost of the project is estimated at $50 million, with funding provided by the Green Climate Fund and other international organizations. The project will be implemented in two phases over a period of ten years, with an initial investment of $10 million.

## Strategic Objectives and Priority Impact Areas

- **Enhance climate technology development and transfer**
- **Support country ownership and access to climate finance**
- **Improve mechanisms for transparency and accountability**

---

More specifically, the programme will aim to address legal, regulatory, and operational challenges to enhance the effectiveness of GCF-funded projects. This includes addressing issues such as:

- The need for enhanced regulatory frameworks
- The harmonization of administrative procedures
- The development of a robust risk management framework

Furthermore, the programme will build confidence among private sector actors by providing technical assistance, enhancing legal capacity, and ensuring that environmental and social safeguards are in place. This will also support the GCF's efforts to enhance its own systems and processes.

### D.2.3

The programme is compatible with the objectives of the Paris Agreement and contributes to the implementation of the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) and the 2030 Agenda for Sustainable Development. It aligns with the GCF's strategic plan, which aims to support countries in their efforts to combat climate change. The programme will also contribute to the achievement of the GCF's objectives by enhancing its own systems and processes.

Sustainable development (item 906 within approximately 2 pages)

The GCF's work includes activities in the following areas:

- **Adaptation** (Code GCF.03) (landscapes and cities, water management)
- **Mitigation** (Code GCF.04) (renewable energy, energy efficiency)
- **Cross-cutting themes** (Code GCF.17) (gender equality & social inclusion)

The programme will also include activities in the following areas:

- **Enhancing climate technology development and transfer**
- **Supporting country ownership and access to climate finance**

## D

### 0.3.1.3 Environmental
The proposal is to establish systems and tools required to monitor and report on emissions, carbon sequestration, and other environmental damage associated with the implementation, handling, and use of the project’s activities. This will include monitoring of greenhouse gas (GHG) emissions from fuel burning devices and their fuel consumption, sustainable pollution control in this context, is a subset of the overall environmental management system. The monitoring systems will be designed to ensure that the project does not cause significant adverse environmental impacts. It is also important to monitor the use of natural resources such as water, soil, and biodiversity.

### 0.3.1.4 Social Development
The proposal includes social development activities resulting from reduced emissions or diesel fuel for electricity generation. These could include health outcomes for students of participating countries. These could also benefit local communities through improved access to education, clean water, and sanitation. The project presents a significant opportunity for these local benefits to be realized by the provision of sustainable energy solutions. It is important that the project is designed in such a way as to ensure that it does not compromise or reduce beneficial projects. This includes ensuring that the project is designed and implemented with the view to complement and enhance existing social development programs.

### 0.3.1.4 Gender
The program will take a proactive agenda to supporting gender equality development. This is important in ensuring that women are included in all aspects of the project implementation, including decision-making processes. It is also important to ensure that women have access to the same opportunities as men and that they are not excluded from participating in the project.

### 0.3.1.5 Indigenous Peoples
The program will take a proactive agenda to supporting indigenous peoples development. This is important in ensuring that indigenous peoples are included in all aspects of the project implementation, including decision-making processes. It is also important to ensure that indigenous peoples have access to the same opportunities as other communities and that they are not excluded from participating in the project.

### 0.3.1.6 Indigenous Peoples
The program will take a proactive agenda to supporting indigenous peoples development. This is important in ensuring that indigenous peoples are included in all aspects of the project implementation, including decision-making processes. It is also important to ensure that indigenous peoples have access to the same opportunities as other communities and that they are not excluded from participating in the project.

### 0.4.1 Next Steps
The next step is to finalize the proposal and seek feedback from key stakeholders. # Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal V.1.5 PAGE 47

## D

Save the planet and provide incentives to address climate challenges. Directing the GCF funds towards the most vulnerable countries is a priority. The GCF will support developing countries in their efforts to address climate change by providing funding for projects that reduce greenhouse gas emissions and enhance resilience to the impacts of climate change. The GCF will also promote the development and transfer of environmentally sound technologies.

### 0.4.3

It is crucial to work with the private sector, given the scale of the challenge. Private investment is essential to meet the needs of developing countries. The GCF should support the development of a pipeline of projects that are ready for implementation by the private sector. This will help to leverage additional resources and ensure that the GCF funds are used effectively.

### 0.5

#### Country ownership (max 300 words, approximately 1 page)

##### Alignment with national priorities

###### 0.5.1

In accordance with Section B, the proposed programme is closely aligned with the proposals identified in the NDCs. It builds on existing programmes and projects that are already underway to support climate change mitigation and adaptation efforts. The GCF will continue to work with countries to ensure that these programmes and projects are implemented effectively.

###### 0.5.2

National Development Strategies (NDS) and other national development plans provide a framework for the alignment of GCF resources with national priorities. These strategies include objectives, targets, and indicators that guide the implementation of climate change mitigation and adaptation activities. The GCF will work closely with countries to ensure that its funding is aligned with these national development plans.

## D.3.1 Base year emissions data on National Reporting Tables are already reported with GCF reporting requirements and can be used to inform the baseline GHG emissions of projects and do not need to be re-reported in the project proposal. The project proposal should include a description of how the project will contribute to reducing GHG emissions, including any necessary related activity analysis. On a change over time basis, as needed, to enable credible tracking of progress towards the project’s objectives. This can be done through regular monitoring and reporting on GHG emissions areas where additional capacity and support could be needed, all of which has informed the design of the programme.

### The role of MoE and other relevant stakeholders

#### D.3.4 Similar to other sector-based programmes, the US$ programme involves a complex process with multiple steps and requires close coordination between different entities. The US$ programme is implemented by the GCF through the National Designated Authorities (NDAs) in collaboration with the relevant ministries, agencies, and institutions. For example, MoE is responsible for the design and implementation of the programme, including the identification of projects, the development of project proposals, and the monitoring and evaluation of the programme’s impact.

#### D.3.5 Moreover, the programme places a strong emphasis on engaging with national stakeholders such as CCREE associations, and communities. Public engagement is placed at the centre of the programme’s design and implementation process to ensure that it is inclusive and responsive to local needs and priorities.

### D.4 Efficiencies and effectiveness (US$ yields, approximately US$ 100)

#### D.4.1 The US$ programme is designed to be cost-effective compared with alternative approaches in the free market. The programme is expected to generate significant co-benefits for the environment and society. The programme will also contribute to achieving the GCF’s objectives of reducing GHG emissions, increasing resilience to climate change, and promoting sustainable development. The programme is expected to achieve these outcomes at a cost-effective price point.

### D.4.2 The mitigation of GHG emissions through the programme is expected to be cost-effective compared with other available options. The programme is designed to achieve significant reductions in GHG emissions at a lower cost than alternative approaches. The programme is also expected to generate significant co-benefits for the environment and society, including improved air quality, increased resilience to climate change, and enhanced sustainable development.
